and make sure the budget is satisfied on average
as of the result
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}g_{t}(\theta_{t})-b\leq o(T)
.
As mentioned in the previous section,
to solve the problem of the increasing difficulty in satisfying all the constraints

g_{t}(\theta^{*})-b_{t}\leq 0,t=\{1,2,\dots,T\}
occurred in previous algorithms,

[
14
]
used another comparator

\hat{\theta}=\operatornamewithlimits{argmin}_{\theta\in\Theta_{K}}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(\theta)
,
where
\Theta_{K}=\{\theta\in\mathcal{S}_{0}:\sum\limits_{i=t}^{t+K-1}g_{i}(\theta)\leq 0,1\leq t\leq T-K+1\}
,

\mathcal{S}_{0}
is the fixed convex set, and
K
is a user-determined parameter.

However, as discussed at the beginning of this chapter,
the constraint set
\Theta_{K}
is not appropriate
in other resource allocation problems such as the job scheduling and rates of failure allocation (the constraint violation itself),
since many applications’ long-term constraint cannot be simply added together.
In general, there are three types of time-dependent long-term constraint:

1.

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}g_{t}(\theta_{t})
, sum of the constraint functions, ideal for non-causal constraint such as budget one,
which is used in
[
14
,
12
,
64
]
.

2.

Q_{T}
, where
Q_{t}=[Q_{t-1}+g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
,
t=1,2,\dots,T

and
Q_{0}=0
, which considers the causality restriction when adding the constraints.
For example, the queuing/job scheduling constraint as mentioned before.
However, previous works dealing with queuing type long-term constraint such as
[
12
,
64
]

usually use
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}g_{t}(\theta_{t})
, which is inappropriate.

3.

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
, cumulative constraint, which only considers the violation part
and is ideal for the long-term failure rate constraint like mistake error.

This section’s goal is to enable our proposed algorithms to apply to different types of
time-dependent long-term constraint problems
by bounding the
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
,
since bounding the 3rd type implies the bound for the other two types.

Since the constraint set
\Theta_{K}
cannot be used when bounding
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
,
we need a new way to solve the
loose regret
due to
the problem of
the increasing difficulty in satisfying all the constraints.
As discussed in the previous chapters, another tighter performance metric used in online learning is called
dynamic regret
,
which measures the difference of the cumulative loss
against a comparison sequence,
z_{1},\ldots,z_{T}\in\mathcal{S}
:

\mathcal{R}_{d}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(z_{t})

For the convex
f_{t}
,
\mathcal{R}_{d}\leq O(\sqrt{T(1+V)})
is obtained by
[
22
]
,
while for the strongly convex or exp-concave
f_{t}
,

\mathcal{R}_{d}\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}
is shown in
[
38
]
,
where
V
is the comparator sequence’s path-length defined as:

\sum_{t=2}^{T}\|z_{t}-z_{t-1}\|\leq V

For the purposes of both solving the problem of the loose bound occurred in
static regret

and mitigating the generalization issue in using the set
\Theta_{K}
,
we extend the Algorithm
9
to bound the dynamic regret
\mathcal{R}_{d}
, where the comparator sequence
z_{1}^{T}

is coming from the set

V_{K}(z_{1}^{T})=\{z_{1}^{T}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}:\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\|z_{t}-z_{t-1}\|\leq V,\text{the number of feasible $z_{i}$ (e.g.,$g_{i}(z_{i})\leq 0$) is K}\}
.
This generalizes the comparator set
\Theta_{K}

by allowing the changes of the comparator sequence as opposed to a fixed one,
which has a much tighter bound compared to the static regret
and is more appropriate under the changing environments.

The assumptions used in this section are the following:

•

The fixed convex set
\mathcal{S}_{0}
is compact with diameter equal to
D
.

•

Both
f_{t}
and
g_{t}
are Lipschitz continuous with
\partial_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta)\leq G
,
and
\partial_{\theta}g_{t}(\theta)\leq G
.
Since
\mathcal{S}_{0}
is compact, without loss of generality,
we assume
f_{t}(\theta)\leq F
,
g_{t}(\theta)\leq F
,
\forall\theta\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
.

•

The comparator sequence
z_{1}^{T}
coming from

V_{K}(z_{1}^{T})=\{z_{1}^{T}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}:\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\|z_{t}-z_{t-1}\|\leq V,\text{the number of feasible $z_{i}$ (e.g.,$g_{i}(z_{i})\leq 0$) is K}\}

is not empty.

where the first two assumptions are ubiquitous in the online convex optimization.
The 3rd one is used to define the dynamic regret used in this chapter,
which is less restrictive compared to
both the
\Theta_{K}=\{\theta\in\mathcal{S}_{0}:\sum\limits_{i=t}^{t+K-1}g_{i}(\theta)\leq 0,1\leq t\leq T-K+1\}
in
[
14
]

and the
\cap_{t}\{\theta\in\mathcal{S}_{0}:g_{t}(\theta)\leq 0\}
in
[
13
,
12
]
.
Since
g_{t}
can be generated adversarially,
it is possible to make
\cap_{t}\{\theta\in\mathcal{S}_{0}:g_{t}(\theta)\leq 0\}
infeasible
by varying the
g_{t}
intentionally.

In order to solve the time-changing long-term constraint
g_{t}(\theta)
,
we modify the Eq. (
46
) as:

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)=f_{t}(\theta)+\lambda[g_{t}(\theta)]_{+}-\frac{\phi_{t}}{2}\lambda^{2}

(57)

Although the analysis in the previous section
can be used to deal with time-changing
g_{t}(\theta)
,
the results only hold true w.r.t. the very loose
static regret
.

24.1
Convex Case

Let us first discuss the update rule and the results associated with the case when
f_{t}(\theta)
is convex.

We first change the
\mathcal{L}_{t}
in Eq. (
57
)
by replacing the time-dependent parameter
\phi_{t}
with
\sigma\eta
as:

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)=f_{t}(\theta)+\lambda[g_{t}(\theta)]_{+}-\frac{\sigma\eta}{2}\lambda^{2}

(58)

The update rule for
t=1,2,\dots,T
is

\displaystyle\lambda_{t}=\frac{[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma\eta}

(59a)

\displaystyle\theta_{t+1}=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}\Big(\theta_{t}-\eta\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\Big)

(59b)

where
\theta_{1}
is initialized in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
, and we abuse the sub-gradient notation
to denote a single element of the sub-gradient by
\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}
.

With the update rule in Eq. (
59
), we can get the following result:

Theorem 5.3
.

For any comparator sequence
z_{1}^{T}\in V_{K}(z_{1}^{T})
, by setting
\sigma=2G^{2}

and
\eta=O(\sqrt{\frac{T-K+1+V}{T}})
, we can bound the
\mathcal{R}_{d}
and
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{d}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\leq O(\sqrt{T(T-K+1+V)})

(60a)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq O(\sqrt{T(T-K+1+V)})

(60b)

Please refer to the Appendix for all the omitted proofs in this section.

Theorem
5.3
generalizes the results in the previous section
by both varying the comparator sequence and the constraint feasibility.
More specifically, Theorem
5.3
recovers the result in previous section
by setting
V=0
and
K=T
.

One direct consequence of the above theorem is:

Corollary 5.1
.

If
T-K=O(T^{1-\epsilon_{1}})
,
\epsilon_{1}\in[0,1]
, and

V=O(T^{1-\epsilon_{2}})
,
\epsilon_{2}\in[0,1]
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{d}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\leq\max\{O(T^{1-\epsilon_{1}/2}),O(T^{1-\epsilon_{2}/2})\}

(61a)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq\max\{O(T^{1-\epsilon_{1}/2}),O(T^{1-\epsilon_{2}/2})\}

(61b)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\leq\max\{O(T^{1-\epsilon_{1}/4}),O(T^{1-\epsilon_{2}/4})\}

(61c)

Proof.

The first two inequalities are due the direct calculation by plugging
T-K=O(T^{1-\epsilon_{1}})

and
V=O(T^{1-\epsilon_{2}})
into Eq. (
60a
) and (
60b
).
The third inequality can be obtained by viewing
[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+},t=1,2,\dots,T
as a vector
and using the vector norm inequality
\|x\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{T}\|x\|
.
∎

The above Corollary generalizes the result in
[
14
]

by considering the dynamic regret w.r.t
z_{1}^{T}
and more general long-term constraint bound.

24.2
Strongly Convex Case

In this case, we use the Augmented Lagrangian function
\mathcal{L}_{t}
defined in Eq. (
57
),
which is rewritten here as:

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)=f_{t}(\theta)+\lambda[g_{t}(\theta)]_{+}-\frac{\phi_{t}}{2}\lambda^{2}

The update rule for
t=1,2,\dots,T
is

\displaystyle\lambda_{t}=\frac{[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\phi_{t}}

(62a)

\displaystyle\theta_{t+1}=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}_{0}}\Big(\theta_{t}-\eta_{t}\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\Big)

(62b)

where
\theta_{1}
is initialized in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
, and we abuse the sub-gradient notation
to denote a single element of the sub-gradient by
\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}
.

Compared to the update rule in Eq. (
59
), the one
in strongly convex case has time-dependent parameters like
\phi_{t}
and
\eta_{t}
.
This is aligned with the parameter setup in previous works
like
[
24
,
13
,
38
]
.

The update rule in Eq. (
62
) results in the following theorem:

Theorem 5.4
.

By using
\phi_{t}=2G^{2}\eta_{t}
,
\eta_{t}=\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell(1-\gamma^{t})}
,
and
\gamma=1-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V+T-K,\log^{2}T/T\}}{(D+1)T}}
,
for
f_{t}
with strong convexity parameter
\ell
and any comparator sequence
z_{1}^{T}\in V_{K}(z_{1}^{T})
, the following results hold:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{d}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\leq\max\{O(\sqrt{T(T-K+V)}),O(\log T)\}

(63a)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\leq\max\{O(T^{3/4}(T-K+V)^{1/4}),O(\sqrt{T\log T})\}

(63b)

Compared to the result in convex case, both the
\mathcal{R}_{d}
and the
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(x_{t})]_{+}

are improved.
The improvement in terms of the order complexity only happens when

K=T
and
V=o(1)
(e.g.,
V=0
).
For the other cases, it also reduces the additive value by about
\sqrt{T}
.

25
Conclusion

In this chapter, we propose algorithms for OCO with both convex and strongly convex objective functions.
By applying different update strategies that utilize a modified augmented Lagrangian function,
they can solve OCO with a squared/clipped long-term constraints requirement.
The algorithm for general convex case provides the useful bounds for
both the long-term constraint violation and the constraint violation
at each time step.
Furthermore, the bounds for the strongly convex case is an improvement compared with the previous efforts in the literature.
Experiments show that our algorithms can follow the constraint boundary tightly and
have relatively smaller clipped long-term constraint violation with reasonably low objective regret.

Furthermore, we extend the algorithms to solve the time-dependent long-term constraint
problem with a variant of
dynamic regret
guarantee,
which can be applied to more general resource allocation problems than the previous algorithms.

Chapter 6
Conclusion

Tracking the changes of the environments is a key difference between Online Convex Optimization (OCO) algorithms
and the batch processing based approaches,
since the sequential data/observation tends to be shifting over time.
In this thesis,
we develop different OCO algorithms
for various problems
to enable the decision making on-the-fly with better adaptivity to the changing environments.

One way to have better adaptivity is to examine the proposed algorithms’ performance by the notion of the
dynamic regret
,
which compares the algorithm’s cumulative loss
against that incurred by a comparison sequence.
For the general exp-concave or strongly convex problems,
we propose discounted Online Newton algorithm to
have dynamic regret guarantee
\mathcal{R}_{d}\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}
,
which is inspired by the forgetting factor used in the Recursive Least Squares algorithms.
Moreover, the trade-off between static and dynamic regret
is analyzed for both Online Least-Squares and its generalization of
strongly convex and smooth objective.
To obtain more computationally efficient algorithms,
we also propose a novel gradient descent step size rule for
strongly convex functions,
which recovers the dynamic regret bounds
described above.

Another way to deal with changing environments is to upper bound the
notion of
adaptive regret
.
Previous literature has developed algorithms
for the online convex problems by running a pool of algorithms in parallel,
resulting in
the unwanted increase in both the running time and the implementation complexity.
To avoid these problems,
we propose a new algorithm with same performance guarantee,
which is the exponentiated gradient
descent algorithm with a mixture of fixed-share step.
We show that this algorithm can be applied
to the online Principal Component Analysis (PCA) and its extension of variance minimization under changing environments.

For the constrained OCO algorithms, a projection operator is almost unavoidable.
When the constrain set is complex,
such operation is very time-consuming and prevents the algorithms from the true online implementation.
To accelerate the OCO algorithms’ update,
our third part of the thesis propose algorithms to replace the true desired projection
with an approximate closed-form one.
Although the approximation may cause constraint violation for some time steps,
sub-linear cumulative constraint violation is guaranteed to achieve the constraint satisfaction on average.
Furthermore, single step constraint violation is bounded to avoid undesired large step violations.
Finally,
we extend our proposed algorithms’ idea to solve the more general time-dependent online resource allocation problems
with performance guarantee by a variant of
dynamic regret
.

Chapter 7
Trading-Off Static and Dynamic Regret in Online Least-Squares
and Beyond

Proof of Lemma
3.4
:

Proof.

The proof follows the analysis in Chapter 2 of
[
65
]
.

From the strong convexity of
f_{t}(\theta)
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{t}(\theta)&\geq f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\\
&=f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})-\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\\
&=f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(64)

According to the optimality condition of the update rule in Eq.(
17
),
we have
\big(\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})\big)^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})\geq 0,\forall\theta\in\mathcal{S}
,
which is
\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})\geq\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})
.
Then combine with Eq.(
64
), we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{t}(\theta)&\geq f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(65)

From the smoothness of
f_{t}(\theta)
, we have

f_{t}(\theta_{t+1})\leq f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})+\frac{u}{2}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}
.
Since
\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}=\frac{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})+u(1-\gamma)}{1-\gamma}\geq u
,
we have
f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t})\geq f_{t}(\theta_{t+1})-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}
.
Then combined with inequality (
65
), we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{t}(\theta)&\geq f_{t}(\theta_{t+1})-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\\
&=f_{t}(\theta_{t+1})+\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(66)

By setting
\theta=\theta_{t}^{*}
and using the fact
f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})\leq f_{t}(\theta_{t+1})
,
we reformulate the above inequality as:

\begin{array}[]{l}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t})\leq-\frac{\ell(1-\gamma)}{2\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})+2u(1-\gamma)}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(67)

Since
\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|^{2}=\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}+\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|^{2}
,
we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|^{2}&=\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|^{2}+2(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t})\\
&\leq\big(1-\frac{\ell(1-\gamma)}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})+u(1-\gamma)}\big)\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|^{2}\\
&\leq\big(1-\frac{\ell(1-\gamma)}{\ell\gamma+u(1-\gamma)}\big)\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(68)

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.4
:

Proof.

We use the same steps as in the previous section.
First, according to the Mean Value Theorem,
we have

f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})=\nabla f_{t}(x)^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})\leq\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
,
where
x\in\{v|v=\delta\theta_{t}+(1-\delta)\theta_{t}^{*},\delta\in[0,1]\}
.
Due to the assumption on the upper bound of the norm of the gradient, we have

f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})\leq G\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.
As a result,

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})\big)\leq G\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.

Now we need to upper bound the term
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
is equal to
\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}+\theta_{t-1}^{*}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
,
which is less than

\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|
.
According to Lemma
3.4
,
we have
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\rho\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
,
where
\rho
is equal to
\sqrt{1-\frac{l(1-\gamma)}{u(1-\gamma)+l\gamma}}
.
Then we have

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|+\rho\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|
,
which can be reformulated as

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho}(\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|++\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|)
.

1-\rho=1-\sqrt{1-\frac{a_{0}}{b_{0}}}=\frac{\sqrt{b_{0}}-\sqrt{b_{0}-a_{0}}}{\sqrt{b_{0}}}
,
where
a_{0}=\ell
and
b_{0}=\frac{\ell\gamma+u(1-\gamma)}{1-\gamma}
.
Thus,
1/(1-\rho)=\frac{\sqrt{b_{0}}}{\sqrt{b_{0}}-\sqrt{b_{0}-a_{0}}}=\frac{\sqrt{b_{0}}(\sqrt{b_{0}}+\sqrt{b_{0}-a_{0}})}{a_{0}}
.
After plugging in the expression of
1-\gamma=1/T^{\beta}
,

1/(1-\rho)=\frac{\sqrt{\ell(T^{\beta}-1)+u}\big(\sqrt{\ell(T^{\beta}-1)+u}+\sqrt{\ell(T^{\beta}-1)+u-\ell}\big)}{\ell}\leq\frac{2\big(\ell(T^{\beta}-1)+u\big)}{\ell}=2(T^{\beta}-1)+u/\ell

Then
\mathcal{R}_{d}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})\big)\leq G\frac{1}{1-\rho}\big(\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|++\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|\big)\leq G\big(2(T^{\beta}-1)+u/\ell\big)\big(\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|++\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|\big)
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.5
:

Proof.

The proof follows the similar steps in the proof of Theorem
3.3
.

According to the non-expansive property of the projection operator and the update rule in Eq.(
17
),
we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}&\leq\left\|\theta_{t}-\eta_{t}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}\\
&=\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}-2\eta_{t}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})+\eta_{t}^{2}\left\|\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}\end{array}

The reformulation gives us

\begin{array}[]{ll}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})&\leq\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\big(\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}\big)+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\left\|\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(69)

Moreover,

f_{t}(\theta^{*})\geq f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta^{*}-\theta_{t})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta^{*}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}

due to strong convexity,
which is equivalent to

\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})\geq f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})+\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta^{*}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}
.
Combined with Eq.(
69
), we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})&\leq\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\big(\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}\big)+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\left\|\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta^{*}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

Summing up from
t=1
to
T
with
\left\|\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}\leq G^{2}
, we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\big)&\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\big(\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}\big)+\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}G^{2}-\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\ell}{2}\left\|\theta^{*}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\\
&\leq G^{2}/2\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\eta_{t}+\frac{1/\eta_{1}-\ell}{2}\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta^{*}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\Big[(\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{t-1}}-\ell)\left\|\theta^{*}-\theta_{t}\right\|^{2}\Big]\end{array}

(70)

Since
\eta_{t}=\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})+u(1-\gamma)}
,
1/\eta_{1}=u

and
\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{t-1}}-\ell=\frac{\ell(\gamma^{t-1}-1)(1-\gamma)}{1-\gamma}\leq 0
.

For the term
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\eta_{t}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})+u(1-\gamma)}
,
it can be reformulated as

\frac{1}{u}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\frac{u(1-\gamma)}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})}}{1+\frac{u(1-\gamma)}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})}}=\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1}{u}\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\frac{\frac{u(1-\gamma)}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})}}{1+\frac{u(1-\gamma)}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})}}\leq\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1}{u}\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\frac{u(1-\gamma)}{\ell(\gamma-\gamma^{t})}=\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell\gamma}\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t-1}}=\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell\gamma}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}
.
For
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}
,
we know that
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}\leq O(T)
as shown in the proof of Theorem
3.3
.
For the term
\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell\gamma}
,
\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell\gamma}=\frac{1}{\ell(T^{\beta}-1)}
.
Combining these two terms’ inequalities,
we get that
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\eta_{t}\leq O(T^{1-\beta})
.

As a result, the inequality (
70
) can be reduced to

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\big)\leq O(T^{1-\beta})

∎

Proof of Corollary
3.3
:

Proof.

Since
\gamma=1-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}
and
V\in[0,2DT]
,

1/2\leq\gamma<1
.

Next, we upper bound each term on the right-hand-side of Theorem
3.6
individually.

\frac{1}{1-\gamma}V=2\sqrt{\frac{2DT}{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}}V\leq O(\sqrt{TV})
.
In order to bound the second term, Lemma
3.3
implies that

(1-\gamma)\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}\leq 1+(1-\gamma)(T+\frac{\ln(1-\gamma)}{\ln\gamma})
.

In this case, the logarithm terms can be bounded by:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\ln(1-\gamma)}{\ln\gamma}&=\frac{-\ln(\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}})}{-\ln(1-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}})}=\frac{-\ln(\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}})}{\ln\Big(1+\frac{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}\Big)}\\
&\leq\ln(2\sqrt{\frac{2DT}{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}})4\sqrt{\frac{2DT}{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}}\leq O(\ln(T/\log T)\frac{T}{\log T})\leq O(T)\end{array}

where the first inequality follows by
\ln(1+x)\geq\frac{1}{2}x,x\in[0,1]
,
and
1-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}<1
.

Thus,
(1-\gamma)\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}
.
The final result follows by adding the two terms.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.5
:

Proof.

The first part of the proof is the same as the first part of the result in the Proof of Lemma 1 in
[
22
]
,
which follows methods of
[
35
]
.
We define
L_{t}^{\gamma}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t}f_{i}(\theta_{i}^{\gamma})
,
and
W_{t}=\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{t}^{\gamma})
.

The following update is equivalent to the update rule in Algorithm
2
:

w_{t}^{\gamma}=\frac{w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{t-1}^{\gamma})}{\sum\limits_{\mu\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\mu}\exp(-\alpha L_{t-1}^{\mu})},\quad t\geq 2.

(71)

First, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\log W_{T}&=\log\big(\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{T}^{\gamma})\big)\\
&\geq\log\big(\max\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{T}^{\gamma})\big)=-\alpha\min\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}\big(L_{T}^{\gamma}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\log\frac{1}{w_{1}^{\gamma}}\big).\end{array}

(72)

Then we bound the quantity
\log(W_{t}/W_{t-1})
.
For
t\geq 2
, we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\log\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{W_{t-1}}\Big)&=\log\Big(\frac{\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{t}^{\gamma})}{\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{t-1}^{\gamma})}\Big)\\
&=\log\Big(\frac{\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{t-1}^{\gamma})\exp(-\alpha f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma}))}{\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha L_{t-1}^{\gamma})}\Big)\\
&=\log\Big(\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{t}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma}))\Big)\par\end{array}

(73)

where the last equality is due to Eq.(
71
).

When
t=1
,
\log W_{1}=\log\Big(\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha f_{1}(\theta_{1}^{\gamma}))\Big)
.
Then
\log W_{T}
can be expressed as:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\log W_{T}=\log W_{1}+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\log\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{W_{t-1}}\Big)=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\log\Big(\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{t}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma}))\Big).\end{array}

(74)

The rest of the proof is new.

Due to the
\alpha
-exp-concavity,

\exp(-\alpha f_{t}(\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{t}^{\gamma}\theta_{t}^{\gamma}))\geq\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{t}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma}))
,
which is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{ll}\log\Big(\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{t}^{\gamma}\exp(-\alpha f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma}))\Big)\leq-\alpha f_{t}\Big(\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}w_{t}^{\gamma}\theta_{t}^{\gamma}\Big)=-\alpha f_{t}(\theta_{t})\end{array}

(75)

Combining the Inequalities (
72
), (
74
), and (
75
),
we get

-\alpha\min\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}\big(L_{T}^{\gamma}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\log\frac{1}{w_{1}^{\gamma}}\big)\leq-\alpha\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(\theta_{t})

which can be reformulated as

\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(\theta_{t})\leq\min\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{H}}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma})+\frac{1}{\alpha}\log\frac{1}{w_{1}^{\gamma}}\Big)

Since it holds for the minimum value, it is true for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{H}
,
which completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.7
:

Proof.

When
\gamma=\gamma^{*}=1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{\frac{T}{\log^{2}T}V,1\}}{2D}}=1-\eta^{*}
, we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T}(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma^{*}})-f_{t}(z_{t}))\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}

based on the Corollary
3.2
.

Since
0\leq V\leq 2TD
,
\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T\sqrt{2D}}\leq\eta^{*}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

According to our definition of
\eta_{i}
,
\min\eta_{i}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T\sqrt{2D}}

and
\frac{1}{2}\leq\max\eta_{i}<1
,
which means for any value of
V
, there always exists a
\eta_{k}
such that

\eta_{k}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T\sqrt{2D}}2^{k-1}\leq\eta^{*}\leq 2\eta_{k}=\eta_{k+1}

where
k=\lfloor\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}(\max\{\frac{T}{\log^{2}T}V,1\})\rfloor+1
.

Now we claim that that running the algorithm with
\gamma_{k}
incurs at
most a constant factor increase in the dynamic regret.

Since
0<\eta_{k}\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}\leq\gamma_{k}=1-\eta_{k}<1
and
\gamma_{k}\geq\gamma^{*}
.

According to Theorem
3.1
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma_{k}})-f_{t}(z_{t}))&\leq-a_{1}T\log\gamma_{k}-a_{2}\log(1-\gamma_{k})\quad+\frac{a_{3}}{1-\gamma_{k}}V+a_{4}.\end{array}

Now we bound each term of the regret in terms of the value obtained
by using
\gamma^{*}
.
For the first term on the RHS,
-T\log\gamma_{k}=T\log\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}\leq T\log\frac{1}{\gamma^{*}}
.

For the second one,
-\log(1-\gamma_{k})=-\log\frac{1}{2}(2-2\gamma_{k})=-\log\frac{1}{2}2\eta_{k}
.
Since
1\geq 2\eta_{k}\geq\eta^{*}
,
\frac{1}{2}2\eta_{k}\geq\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}
,
which leads to
-\log\frac{1}{2}2\eta_{k}\leq-\log\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}

and
-\log(1-\gamma_{k})\leq-\log\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}=\log 2-\log(1-\gamma^{*})
.

For the third one,
\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{k}}V=\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}V=\frac{2}{2\eta_{k}}V\leq\frac{2}{\eta^{*}}V=\frac{2}{1-\gamma^{*}}V
.
Thus the claim has been proved.

Since using
\gamma_{k}
in place of
\gamma^{*}
increases the
regret by at most a constant factor, Corollary
3.2

implies that:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma_{k}})-f_{t}(z_{t}))\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}

(76)

Furthermore, from Lemma
3.5
we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma_{k}}))&\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\log\frac{1}{w_{1}^{\gamma_{k}}}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\log(k(k+1))\\
&\leq 2\frac{1}{\alpha}\log(k+1)\leq O(\log(\log T))\end{array}

(77)

Combining the above inequalities (
76
) and (
77
) completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.6
:

Proof.

Let
g(x)=\exp(-\alpha f(x))
. To prove the concavity of
g(x)
,
it is equivalent to show
\langle\nabla g(x)-\nabla g(y),x-y\rangle\leq 0,x,y\in\mathcal{S}
.
Since
\nabla g(x)=\exp(-\alpha f(x))(-\alpha)\nabla f(x)
,
it is equivalent to prove that

\langle\exp(-\alpha f(x))\nabla f(x)-\exp(-\alpha f(y))\nabla f(y),x-y\rangle\geq 0
,
which can be reformulated as

\exp(-\alpha f(x))\langle\nabla f(x),x-y\rangle\geq\exp(-\alpha f(y))\langle\nabla f(y),x-y\rangle

(78)

Without loss of generality, let us assume
f(x)\geq f(y)
.
Due to
\ell
-strong convexity,
f(x)\geq f(y)+\langle\nabla f(y),x-y\rangle+\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}
,
which leads to

\langle\nabla f(y),x-y\rangle\leq f(x)-f(y)-\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}

(79)

What’s more,
f(y)\geq f(x)+\langle\nabla f(x),y-x\rangle+\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}
,
which leads to

\langle\nabla f(x),x-y\rangle\geq f(x)-f(y)+\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}

(80)

The examination of the inequalities (
78
), (
79
), and (
80
)
shows that
it is enough to prove

\exp(-\alpha f(x))(f(x)-f(y)+\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2})\geq\exp(-\alpha f(y))(f(x)-f(y)-\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2})
,
which can be reformulated as

\frac{\ell}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}(\exp(-\alpha f(x))+\exp(-\alpha f(y)))\geq(f(x)-f(y))(\exp(-\alpha f(y))-\exp(-\alpha f(x)))
.
When
x-y=0
, it is always true. Let us consider the case when
\|x-y\|>0
.
Then we need to show that

\frac{\ell}{2}\Big(1+\exp\big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\big)\Big)\geq\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{\|x-y\|}\frac{\exp\Big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\Big)-1}{\|x-y\|}
.
Due to bounded gradient and Mean value theorem,
\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{\|x-y\|}\leq G
,
which means it is enough to show that

\frac{\ell}{2G}\Big(1+\exp\big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\big)\Big)\geq\frac{\exp\Big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\Big)-1}{\|x-y\|}

(81)

According to the Taylor series,

\exp\Big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\Big)=1+\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)+\frac{1}{2!}\alpha^{2}\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)^{2}+\dots+\frac{1}{n!}\alpha^{n}\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)^{n},n\to\infty
.
Thus,
\frac{\exp\Big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\Big)-1}{\|x-y\|}=\alpha\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{\|x-y\|}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{2}(f(x)-f(y))\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{\|x-y\|}+\dots+\frac{1}{n!}\alpha^{n}\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)^{n-1}\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{\|x-y\|},n\to\infty
.
Since
\frac{f(x)-f(y)}{\|x-y\|}\leq G
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\exp\Big(\alpha\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)\Big)-1}{\|x-y\|}\leq\alpha G+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{2}(f(x)-f(y))G+\dots+\frac{1}{n!}\alpha^{n}\big(f(x)-f(y)\big)^{n-1}G\end{array}

(82)

For the LHS of inequality (
81
), it is equal to

\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\ell}{G}+\alpha\frac{\ell}{2G}(f(x)-f(y))+\frac{1}{2!}\alpha^{2}\frac{\ell}{2G}(f(x)-f(y))^{2}+\dots+\frac{1}{n!}\alpha^{n}\frac{\ell}{2G}(f(x)-f(y))^{n},n\to\infty\end{array}

(83)

If we compare the coefficients of the RHS from the inequality (
82
) with the one in (
83
)
and plug in
\alpha=\ell/G^{2}
, we see that it is always smaller or equal,
which completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.8
:

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
3.7
, all we need to show is that
there exists an algorithm
A^{\gamma}
,
which can bound the regret
\sum_{t=1}^{T}(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma})-f_{t}(z_{t}))\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}
.

When
\gamma=\gamma^{*}=1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{\frac{T}{\log^{2}T}V,1\}}{2D}}=1-\eta^{*}
, we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T}(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma^{*}})-f_{t}(z_{t}))\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}

based on the Corollary
3.3
.

Since
0\leq V\leq 2TD
,
\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T\sqrt{2D}}\leq\eta^{*}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

According to our definition of
\eta_{i}
,
\min\eta_{i}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T\sqrt{2D}}

and
\frac{1}{2}\leq\max\eta_{i}<1
,
which means for any value of
V
, there always exists a
\eta_{k}
such that

\eta_{k}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log T}{T\sqrt{2D}}2^{k-1}\leq\eta^{*}\leq 2\eta_{k}=\eta_{k+1}

where
k=\lfloor\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}(\max\{\frac{T}{\log^{2}T}V,1\})\rfloor+1
.

Since
0<\eta_{k}\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}\leq\gamma_{k}=1-\eta_{k}<1
and
\gamma_{k}\geq\gamma^{*}
.

According to Theorem
3.6
, we have

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma_{k}})-f_{t}(z_{t})\big)\leq\frac{2D\ell}{1-\gamma_{k}}V+\frac{G^{2}}{\ell}(1-\gamma_{k})\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{k}^{t}}

For the first term on the RHS,
\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{k}}V=\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}V=\frac{2}{2\eta_{k}}V\leq\frac{2}{\eta^{*}}V=\frac{2}{1-\gamma^{*}}V
.

For the second one,
1-\gamma_{k}\leq 1-\gamma^{*}
.
According to the proof in Corollary
3.3
,

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{k}^{t}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{k}}+T+\frac{\log(1-\gamma_{k})}{\log\gamma_{k}}
.

\frac{\log(1-\gamma_{k})}{\log\gamma_{k}}=\frac{\log\eta_{k}}{\log(1-\eta_{k})}=\frac{-\log\eta_{k}}{-\log(1-\eta_{k})}.

(84)

Since
\eta_{k}\geq\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}
,

\log\eta_{k}\geq\log\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}

and

0<-\log\eta_{k}\leq-\log\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}=\log 2-\log\eta^{*}.

(85)

Since
\eta_{k}\geq\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}
,
1-\eta_{k}\leq 1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*}
.
Then
\log(1-\eta_{k})\leq\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})
,
which results in

-\log(1-\eta_{k})\geq-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})>0.

(86)

Combining inequalities (
85
) and (
86
) with Eq.(
84
),
we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\log(1-\gamma_{k})}{\log\gamma_{k}}\leq\frac{\log 2-\log\eta^{*}}{-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})}=\frac{\log 2}{-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})}+\frac{-\log\eta^{*}}{-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})}\end{array}

(87)

For the first term on the RHS,

\begin{array}[]{ll}-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})&=\log\Big(\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}\Big)=\log\Big(1+\frac{\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}{1-\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}\Big)\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}{1-\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}}\geq\frac{1}{8}\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}\end{array}

where the first inequality is due to
\log(1+x)\geq\frac{1}{2}x,x\in[0,1]
and
the second one is due to
\sqrt{\frac{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}{2DT}}>0
.
As a result,

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\log 2}{-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})}&\leq 8\sqrt{\frac{2DT}{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}}\log 2\leq 8\frac{T}{\log T}\sqrt{2D}\log 2<O(T).\end{array}

For the second term on the RHS of Eq.(
87
),

\begin{array}[]{ll}-\log\eta^{*}=\log\Big(2\sqrt{\frac{2DT}{\max\{V,\log^{2}T/T\}}}\Big)\leq\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\log 2D+\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{T}{\log T}\end{array}

Combining the inequalities for
-\log\eta^{*}
and
-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})
,
we get

\frac{-\log\eta^{*}}{-\log(1-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{*})}\leq(\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\log 2D+\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{T}{\log T})8\frac{T}{\log T}\sqrt{2D}\leq O(T)
.

As a result,
\frac{\log(1-\gamma_{k})}{\log\gamma_{k}}\leq O(T)
and

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma_{k}^{t}}\leq O(T)
.

Since using
\gamma_{k}
does not increase the order when used in place of
\gamma^{*}
,
we get

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{\gamma_{k}})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\leq\max\{O(\log T),O(\sqrt{TV})\}

which combining with the result of Lemma
3.5
completes the proof.

∎

Online Least-Squares Optimization

Consider the online least-squares problem with:

f_{t}(\theta)=\frac{1}{2}\left\|y_{t}-A_{t}\theta\right\|^{2}

(88)

where
A_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
,
A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}
has full rank with
lI\preceq A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}\preceq uI
,
and
y_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
comes from a bounded set with

\left\|y_{t}\right\|\leq D
.

In Chapter
3
, we analyzed the dynamic regret of discounted recursive least squares against comparison
sequences
z_{1},\ldots,z_{T}
with a path length constraint

\sum_{t=2}^{T}\|z_{t}-z_{t-1}\|\leq V
. Additionally, we analyzed the trade-off between static and
dynamic regret of a gradient descent rule with comparison sequence

\theta_{t}^{*}=\operatornamewithlimits{argmin}_{\theta\in\mathcal{S}}f_{t}(\theta)
. In this appendix,
we analyze the trade-off between static regret and dynamic regret with
comparison sequence
\theta_{t}^{*}
achieved by discounted recursive
least squares. We will see that the discounted recursive least squares
achieves trade-offs dependent on the condition number,
\delta=u/l
. In
particular, low dynamic regret is only guaranteed for low condition
numbers.

Recall that discounted recursive least squares corresponds to
Algorithm
1
running with a full Newton step and
\eta=1
.
In this case,
P_{t}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t+1-i}^{\top}A_{t+1-i}=\gamma P_{t-1}+A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}
, and the update rule can be written more explicitly
as

\theta_{t+1}=\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t+1-i}^{\top}A_{t+1-i}\Big)^{-1}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t+1-i}^{\top}y_{t+1-i}\Big)

(89)

The above update rule can be reformulated as:

\theta_{t+1}=\theta_{t}-P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t}).

(90)

Before we analyze dynamic and static regret for the update (
90
),
we first show some supporting results for
\left\|y_{t}-A_{t}x\right\|

and
\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|
, where
x\in\{v|v=\beta\theta_{t}+(1-\beta)\theta_{t}^{*},\beta\in[0,1]\}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\theta_{t}
be the result of Eq.(
90
), and
\theta_{t}^{*}=\operatornamewithlimits{argmin}f_{t}(\theta)
.
For
x\in\{v|v=\beta\theta_{t}+(1-\beta)\theta_{t}^{*},\beta\in[0,1]\}
,
If
\left\|y_{t}\right\|\leq D
, then
\left\|y_{t}-A_{t}x\right\|\leq(u/l+1)D
.

Proof.

\left\|y_{t}-A_{t}x\right\|\leq\left\|A_{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|x\right\|+\left\|y_{t}\right\|
, and

\left\|A_{t}\right\|_{2}=\sqrt{\sigma_{1}(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})}\leq\sqrt{u}
.
For
\left\|x\right\|
, we have

\left\|x\right\|=\left\|\beta\theta_{t}+(1-\beta)\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\beta\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|+(1-\beta)\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.

For the term
\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|
,

\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|=\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\Big)^{-1}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}y_{t-i}\Big)\right\|
,
which can be upper bounded by

\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\Big)^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}y_{t-i}\Big)\right\|
.
Then we upper bound these two terms individually.

\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\Big)^{-1}\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i})}
.
Since
lI\preceq A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\preceq uI
,

\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}lI\preceq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i})\preceq\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}uI
.
Thus,
\sigma_{n}(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i})\geq l\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}
,
which results in
\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\Big)^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{l(1-\gamma^{t-1})}
.

For the term
\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}y_{t-i}\Big)\right\|
,
we have
\left\|\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}y_{t-i}\Big)\right\|\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}\left\|A_{t-i}^{\top}y_{t-i}\right\|\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}\left\|A_{t-i}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|y_{t-i}\right\|\leq\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}\sqrt{u}D
.
Then we have
\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{u}}{l}D
.

For
\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
,
we have
\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|=\left\|(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}\right\|\leq\left\|(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|A_{t}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|y_{t}\right\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{u}}{l}D
. Thus,
\left\|x\right\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{u}}{l}D

and
\left\|y_{t}-A_{t}x\right\|\leq\left\|A_{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|x\right\|+\left\|y_{t}\right\|\leq(u/l+1)D
.

∎

Corollary 7.1
.

Let
\theta_{t}
be the result of Eq.(
90
) and
\theta_{t}^{*}=\operatornamewithlimits{argmin}f_{t}(\theta)
.
For
x\in\{v|v=\beta\theta_{t}+(1-\beta)\theta_{t}^{*},\beta\in[0,1]\}
, we have

\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|\leq\sqrt{u}(u/l+1)D
.

Proof.

For
\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|
, we have
\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|=\left\|A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}x-A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}\right\|\leq\left\|A_{t}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\left\|A_{t}x-y_{t}\right\|\leq\sqrt{u}(u/l+1)D
,
where the second inequality is due to Lemma
7.1
and the assumption of
A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}\preceq uI
.

∎

Moreover, we need to obtain the relationship between
\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}
and
\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}

as another necessary step to get the dynamic regret.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\theta_{t}^{*}
be the solution to
f_{t}(\theta)
in Eq.(
88
).
When we use the discounted recursive least-squares update in Eq.(
90
),
the following relationship is obtained:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}&=\big(I-\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}\big)(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})\\
&=\big(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}\big)^{-1}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})\end{array}

Proof.

If we set
\Phi_{t}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t+1-i}^{\top}y_{t+1-i}=\gamma\Phi_{t-1}+A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}
,
then according to the update of
\theta_{t+1}
in Eq.(
89
),
we have
\theta_{t+1}=(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}+\gamma P_{t-1})^{-1}(A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}+\gamma\Phi_{t-1})
,
which by the use of inverse lemma can be further reformulated as:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{t+1}=\Big(\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}-\gamma^{-2}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1}\Big)\big(A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}+\gamma\Phi_{t-1}\big)\end{array}

(91)

Then for
\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}=\theta_{t+1}-(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}
, we have:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}&=\underbrace{\big(I-\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}\big)}_{\hbox to12.05pt{\vbox to12.05pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.02568pt\lower-6.02568pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}

}

&\quad-\underbrace{\big(\gamma^{-2}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1}-(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\big)A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}}_{\hbox to16.97pt{\vbox to16.97pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.48271pt\lower-8.48271pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\end{array}

(92)

We want to prove
\hbox to20.1pt{\vbox to20.1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\quad\lower-10.05107pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+\hbox to20.1pt{\vbox to20.1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\quad\lower-10.05107pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(-\theta_{t}^{*})=\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.54 0 C 9.54 5.27 5.27 9.54 0 9.54 C -5.27 9.54 -9.54 5.27 -9.54 0 C -9.54 -5.27 -5.27 -9.54 0 -9.54 C 5.27 -9.54 9.54 -5.27 9.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(-(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}y_{t})=\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.54 0 C 9.54 5.27 5.27 9.54 0 9.54 C -5.27 9.54 -9.54 5.27 -9.54 0 C -9.54 -5.27 -5.27 -9.54 0 -9.54 C 5.27 -9.54 9.54 -5.27 9.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
.

Since
A(I+BA)^{-1}B=AB(I+AB)^{-1}=(I+AB)^{-1}AB
, for any compatible matrix
A
and
B
, we have:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.54 0 C 9.54 5.27 5.27 9.54 0 9.54 C -5.27 9.54 -9.54 5.27 -9.54 0 C -9.54 -5.27 -5.27 -9.54 0 -9.54 C 5.27 -9.54 9.54 -5.27 9.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}&=-\big[I-\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}\big](A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}\\
&=-\big[I-(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}\big](A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}\\
&=-\big[(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}-(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}\big]A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}\end{array}

(93)

Also, for any compatible
P
, we have
(I+P)^{-1}=I-(I+P)^{-1}P
.
Then
(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}=I-(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}
.
Then
\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.54 0 C 9.54 5.27 5.27 9.54 0 9.54 C -5.27 9.54 -9.54 5.27 -9.54 0 C -9.54 -5.27 -5.27 -9.54 0 -9.54 C 5.27 -9.54 9.54 -5.27 9.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=-\big[(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}+(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\gamma^{-2}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1}\big]A_{t}^{\top}y_{t}
.
Compared with
\hbox to20.1pt{\vbox to20.1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\quad\lower-10.05107pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 13.63 0 C 13.63 7.53 7.53 13.63 0 13.63 C -7.53 13.63 -13.63 7.53 -13.63 0 C -13.63 -7.53 -7.53 -13.63 0 -13.63 C 7.53 -13.63 13.63 -7.53 13.63 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.3889pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+\hbox to20.1pt{\vbox to20.1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\quad\lower-10.05107pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 13.63 0 C 13.63 7.53 7.53 13.63 0 13.63 C -7.53 13.63 -13.63 7.53 -13.63 0 C -13.63 -7.53 -7.53 -13.63 0 -13.63 C 7.53 -13.63 13.63 -7.53 13.63 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.3889pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
, we need to prove

(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})^{-1}\gamma^{-2}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1}

is equal to

\gamma^{-2}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1}
, which is always true.

As a result, we have
\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}=\big(I-\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(I+A_{t}\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top})^{-1}A_{t}\big)(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})
,
which can be simplified as
\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}=\big(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}\big)^{-1}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})
.

∎

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
\theta_{t}^{*}
be the solution to
f_{t}(\theta)
in Eq.(
88
).
When we use the discounted recursive least-squares update in Eq.(
90
),
the following relation is obtained:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|&\leq\sqrt{\frac{u}{l}}\frac{u\gamma}{u\gamma+l(1-\gamma)}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\end{array}

Proof.

From Lemma
7.2
we know that

\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}=\Big(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}\Big)^{-1}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})

which can be reformulated as:

\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}=P_{t-1}^{-1/2}(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})^{-1}P_{t-1}^{1/2}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})

which gives us the following inequality:

\begin{array}[]{l}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\left\|P_{t-1}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\left\|P_{t-1}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\end{array}

Then we will upper bound the terms on the right-hand side individually.

Since
lI\preceq A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\preceq uI
,

\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}lI\preceq P_{t-1}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{t-1}\gamma^{i-1}A_{t-i}^{\top}A_{t-i}\preceq\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}uI
.

For the term
\left\|P_{t-1}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}
,
we have
\left\|P_{t-1}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1})}}
.
Since
\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1})\geq\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}l
,

\left\|P_{t-1}^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{l}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t-1}}}
.

For the term
\left\|P_{t-1}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}
,
we have
\left\|P_{t-1}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

=

\sqrt{\sigma_{1}(P_{t-1})}
.
Since
\sigma_{1}(P_{t-1})\leq\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}u
,

\left\|P_{t-1}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{u}\sqrt{\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}}
.

For
\left\|(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})^{-1}\right\|_{2}
,
we have
\left\|(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})^{-1}\right\|_{2}

=1/\sigma_{n}(I
+
\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})
.
For the term

\sigma_{n}(I
+
\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})
,
it is equal to

1+\sigma_{n}(\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})

\geq

1+\gamma^{-1}\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1}^{-1/2})\sigma_{n}(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1}^{-1/2})
.

Since
\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1}^{-1/2})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma_{1}(P_{t-1})}}

and
\sigma_{1}(P_{t-1})\leq\frac{1-\gamma^{t-1}}{1-\gamma}u
,

\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1}^{-1/2})\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{u}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t-1}}}
.
Together with
\sigma_{n}(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})\geq l
, we have

\sigma_{n}(P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})\geq\frac{l}{u}\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t-1}}
,
which results in

\left\|(I+\gamma^{-1}P_{t-1}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t-1}^{-1/2})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+\gamma^{-1}\frac{l}{u}\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t-1}}}
.

Combining the above three terms’ inequalities results in

\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{u}{l}}\frac{u(\gamma-\gamma^{t})}{u(\gamma-\gamma^{t})+l(1-\gamma)}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{u}{l}}\frac{u\gamma}{u\gamma+l(1-\gamma)}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|.

∎

Now we are ready to present the dynamic regret for the general recursive least-squares update:

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\theta_{t}^{*}
be the solution to
f_{t}(\theta)
in Eq.(
88
)
and
\delta=u/l\geq 1
be the condition number.
When using the discounted recursive least-squares update in Eq.(
90
)
with
\gamma<\frac{1}{\delta^{3/2}-\delta+1}

and
\rho=\sqrt{\frac{u}{l}}\frac{u\gamma}{u\gamma+l(1-\gamma)}<1
,
we can upper bound the dynamic regret:

\mathcal{R}_{d}\leq\sqrt{u}(u/l+1)D\frac{1}{1-\rho}\big(\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|++\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|\big)

Proof.

The proof follows the similar steps in the proof of Theorem
3.2
.
First, we use the Mean Value Theorem to get

f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})=\nabla f_{t}(x)^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*})\leq\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
,
where
x\in\{v|v=\beta\theta_{t}+(1-\beta)\theta_{t}^{*},\beta\in[0,1]\}
.
According to Corollary
7.1
,

\left\|\nabla f_{t}(x)\right\|\leq\sqrt{u}(u/l+1)D
.
As a result,

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})\big)\leq\sqrt{u}(u/l+1)D\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.

Now we need to upper bound the term
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|&=\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}+\theta_{t-1}^{*}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\\
&\leq\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T-1}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|\\
&\leq\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|.\end{array}

According to Corollary
7.2
,

\left\|\theta_{t+1}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\rho\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|
.
Then the above inequality can be reformulated as

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t}^{*}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho}(\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|++\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|)
.
Then
\mathcal{R}_{d}=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta_{t}^{*})\big)\leq\sqrt{u}(u/l+1)D\frac{1}{1-\rho}(\left\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\right\|++\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\theta_{t}^{*}-\theta_{t-1}^{*}\right\|)
.

∎

In the above Theorem
7.1
, the valid range of
\gamma
is in
(0,1/(\delta^{3/2}-\delta+1))
.
Let us now examine the requirement of
\gamma
to achieve the sub-linear static regret:

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
\theta^{*}
be the solution to
\min\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}(\theta)
.
When using the discounted recursive least-squares update in Eq.(
90
) with
1-\gamma=1/T^{\alpha},\alpha\in(0,1)
,
we can upper bound the static regret:

\mathcal{R}_{s}\leq O(T^{1-\alpha})

Proof.

The proof follows the analysis of the online Newton method
[
24
]
.
From the update in Eq.(
90
),
we have
\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*}=\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}-P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})

and
P_{t}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})=P_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})-\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})
.
Multiplying the two equalities, we have

(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})^{\top}P_{t}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})=(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}P_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})-2\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})+\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})
.

After the reformulation, we have

\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})=\frac{1}{2}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}P_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})-\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})^{\top}P_{t}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})\leq\frac{1}{2}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}P_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})-\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})^{\top}\gamma P_{t}(\theta_{t+1}-\theta^{*})
.

Summing the above inequality from
t=1
to
T
, we have:

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})\\
\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{1}-\theta^{*})^{\top}P_{1}(\theta_{1}-\theta^{*})+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}(P_{t}-\gamma P_{t-1})(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})\\
\hskip 8.50012pt-\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{T+1}-\theta^{*})^{\top}\gamma P_{T}(\theta_{T+1}-\theta^{*})\\
\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{1}-\theta^{*})^{\top}(P_{1}-A_{1}^{\top}A_{1})(\theta_{1}-\theta^{*})+\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}).\end{array}

Since
P_{1}=A_{1}^{\top}A_{1}
and
f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})=\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})-\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})
,
we reformulate the above inequality as:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\Big)&=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})-\frac{1}{2}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})^{\top}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}(\theta_{t}-\theta^{*})\Big)\\
&\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})^{\top}P_{t}^{-1}\nabla f_{t}(\theta_{t})\\
&=\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}(A_{t}\theta_{t}-y_{t})^{\top}A_{t}P_{t}^{-1}A_{t}^{\top}(A_{t}\theta_{t}-y_{t})\\
&\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{1}(P_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t}^{-1/2})\left\|A_{t}\theta_{t}-y_{t}\right\|^{2}\end{array}

(94)

Since
\sigma_{1}(P_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t}^{-1/2})\leq\sigma_{1}(P_{t}^{-1})\sigma_{1}(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})=\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}(P_{t})}\sigma_{1}(A_{t}^{\top}A_{t})
.
Based on the proof of Corollary
7.2
,

\sigma_{n}(P_{t})\geq\frac{1-\gamma^{t}}{1-\gamma}l
and
\sigma_{1}(A_{t}^{T}A_{t})\leq u
.
Then
\sigma_{1}(P_{t}^{-1/2}A_{t}^{\top}A_{t}P_{t}^{-1/2})\leq\frac{u}{l}\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t}}
.
As a result, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\Big)\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\frac{u}{l}\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t}}\left\|A_{t}\theta_{t}-y_{t}\right\|^{2}\leq\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{2}\frac{u}{l}\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma^{t}}(u/l+1)^{2}D^{2}\leq O(T^{1-\alpha})\end{array}

(95)

where the second inequality is due to Lemma
7.1

and the third inequality is due to the fact that
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}1/(1-\gamma^{t})\leq O(T)

as shown in the proof of Theorem
3.3
.

∎

Recall that the valid range of
\gamma
in Theorem
7.1
is

(0,1/(\delta^{3/2}-\delta+1))
,
while having sub-linear static regret requires
\gamma=\frac{T^{\alpha}-1}{T^{\alpha}}
.
Although for some specific
T
, there might be some intersection.
In general, these two are contradictory.
However, as discussed in the main body of the Chapter
3
, more flexible
trade-offs between static and dynamic regret can be achieved via the
gradient descent rule.

Chapter 8
Online Adaptive Principal Component Analysis and Its extensions

Before presenting the proofs, we need the following lemma from previous literature:

Lemma 8.1
.

[
34
]

Suppose
0\leq L\leq\tilde{L}
and
0<R\leq\tilde{R}
. Let
\beta=g(\tilde{L}/\tilde{R})

where
g(z)=1/(1+\sqrt{2/z})
. Then

\frac{-L\ln\beta+R}{1-\beta}\leq L+\sqrt{2\tilde{L}\tilde{R}}+R

Additionally, we need the following classic bound on traces for
postive semidefinite matrices. See, e.g.
[
8
]
.

Lemma 8.2
.

For any positive semi-definite matrix
A
and any symmetric matrices
B
and
C
,

B\preceq C
implies
\mathrm{Tr}(AB)\leq\mathrm{Tr}(AC)
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
:

Proof.

Fix
1\leq r\leq s\leq T
. We set
\mathbf{q_{t}}=\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{n-k}^{n}
for
t=r,\dots,s
and
0
elsewhere. Thus, we
have that
\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\|_{1}
is either
0
or
1
.

According to Lemma
4.1
, for both cases of
\mathbf{q_{t}}
, we have

\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}\mathbf{w_{t}}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}(1-\exp(-\eta))-\eta\mathbf{q_{t}}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{t,i}\ln(\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}})

(96)

The analysis for
\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{t,i}\ln(\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}})
follows the Proof of
Proposition
2
in
[
6
]
.
We describe the steps for completeness, since it is helpful for
understanding the effect of the fixed-share step,
Eq.(
25b
). This analysis will be crucial for the
understanding how the fixed-share step can be applied to PCA
problems.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{t,i}\ln(\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}})=&\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(q_{t,i}\ln\frac{1}{\hat{w}_{t,i}}-q_{t-1,i}\ln\frac{1}{v_{t,i}}\Big)}_{A}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(q_{t-1,i}\ln\frac{1}{v_{t,i}}-q_{t,i}\ln\frac{1}{v_{t+1,i}}\Big)}_{B}\end{array}

(97)

For the expression of
A
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}A=\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}\geq q_{t-1,i}}\Big((q_{t,i}-q_{t-1,i})\ln\frac{1}{\hat{w}_{t,i}}+q_{t-1,i}\ln\frac{v_{t,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}}\Big)+\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}<q_{t-1,i}}\Big(\underbrace{(q_{t,i}-q_{t-1,i})\ln\frac{1}{v_{t,i}}}_{\leq 0}+q_{t,i}\ln\frac{v_{t,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}}\Big)\end{array}

Based on the update in Eq.(
25
), we have
1/\hat{w}_{t,i}\leq n/\alpha

and
v_{t,i}/\hat{w}_{t,i}\leq 1/(1-\alpha)
. Plugging the bounds into the above equation, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}A\leq\underbrace{\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}\geq q_{t-1,i}}(q_{t,i}-q_{t-1,i})}_{=D_{TV}(\mathbf{q_{t}},\mathbf{q_{t-1}})}\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+\underbrace{\Big(\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}\geq q_{t-1,i}}q_{t-1,i}+\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}<q_{t-1,i}}q_{t,i}\Big)}_{=\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}-D_{TV}(\mathbf{q_{t}},\mathbf{q_{t-1}})}\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}.\end{array}

Telescoping the expression of
B
, substituting the above inequality in Eq.(
97
),
and summing over
t=2,\dots,T
, we have

\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}q_{t,i}\ln\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}}\leq m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+\Big(\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}-m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\Big)\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}q_{1,i}\ln\frac{1}{v_{2,i}}.

Adding the
t=1
term to the above inequality, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}q_{t,i}\ln\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{\hat{w}_{t,i}}\leq\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}\ln(n)+m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+\Big(\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}-m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\Big)\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}.\end{array}

Now we bound the right side, using the choices for
\mathbf{q_{t}}

described at the beginning of the proof.
If
r\geq 2
,
m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})=1
, and
\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}=0
.
If
r=1
,
m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})=0
, and
\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}=1
.
Thus,
m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})+\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}=1
, and the right part can be upper bounded by

\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+T\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
.

After combining the above inequality with Eq.(
96
),
setting
\mathbf{q_{t}}=\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{n-k}}^{\text{n}}
for
t=r,\dots,s
and
0
elsewhere,
and multiplying both sides by
n-k
,
we have

\begin{array}[]{l}(1-\exp(-\eta))\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{w_{t}}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}-\eta\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}\leq(n-k)\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+(n-k)T\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\end{array}

If we set
\alpha=1/(1+(n-k)T)
, then the right part can be upper bounded by
(n-k)\ln(n(1+(n-k)T))+1
,
which equals to
D
as defined in the Theorem
4.1
.
Thus, the above inequality can be reformulated as

\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{w_{t}}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}\leq\frac{\eta\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}+D}{1-\exp(-\eta)}

Since the above inequality holds for arbitrary
\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{n-k}}^{\text{n}}
, we have

\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{w_{t}}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}\leq\frac{\eta\min\limits_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{n-k}}^{\text{n}}}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}+D}{1-\exp(-\eta)}

(98)

We will apply the inequality in Lemma
8.1
to upper bound the right part in Eq.(
98
).
With
\min\limits_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{n-k}}^{\text{n}}}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}\leq L

and
\eta=\ln(1+\sqrt{2D/L})
, we have

\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{w_{t}}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}-\min\limits_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{n-k}}^{\text{n}}}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}(n-k)\mathbf{q}^{\top}\mathbf{\ell_{t}}\leq\sqrt{2LD}+D

Since the above inequality always holds for all intervals,
[r,s]
,
the result is proved by maximizing the left side over
[r,s]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
:

Proof.

In the proof, we will examine two cases of
Q_{t}
:
Q_{t}\in\mathscr{B}_{n-k}^{n}
, and
Q_{t}=0
.

We first apply the eigendecomposition to
Q_{t}
as
Q_{t}=\widetilde{D}\operatornamewithlimits{diag}(\mathbf{q_{t}})\widetilde{D}^{\top}
,
where
\widetilde{D}=[\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{1}},\dots,\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{n}}]
.
Since in the adaptive setting,
Q_{t-1}
is either equal to
Q_{t}
or
0
,
they share the same eigenvectors and

Q_{t-1}
can be expressed as
Q_{t-1}=\widetilde{D}\operatornamewithlimits{diag}(\mathbf{q_{t-1}})\widetilde{D}^{\top}
.

According to Lemma
4.2
, the following inequality is true for both cases of
Q_{t}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}\mathrm{Tr}(W_{t}\mathbf{x_{t}}\mathbf{x_{t}}^{\top})(1-\exp(-\eta))-\eta\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\mathbf{x_{t}}\mathbf{x_{t}}^{\top})\leq-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln\widehat{W}_{t})+\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})\end{array}

(99)

The next steps extend proof of Proposition 2 in
[
6
]
to the matrix case.

We analyze the right part of the above inequality,
which can be expressed as:

\begin{array}[]{l}-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln\widehat{W}_{t})+\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})=\bar{A}+\bar{B}\end{array}

(100)

where
\bar{A}=-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln\widehat{W}_{t})+\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t-1}\ln V_{t})
,
and
\bar{B}=-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t-1}\ln V_{t})+\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})
.

We will first upper bound the
\bar{A}
term,
and then telescope the
\bar{B}
term.

can be expressed as:

\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}<q_{t-1,i}}\Big(\underbrace{-\mathrm{Tr}\big((q_{t,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}-q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top})\ln V_{t}\big)}_{\hbox to11.27pt{\vbox to11.27pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.63383pt\lower-5.63383pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

For
1
, it can be expressed as:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}&=\mathrm{Tr}\big((q_{t,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}-q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}^{T}})\ln\widehat{W}_{t}^{-1}\big)\\
&\leq\mathrm{Tr}\big((q_{t,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}-q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top})\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}\big)=(q_{t,i}-q_{t-1,i})\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}.\end{array}

The inequality holds because the update in Eq.(
32b
)
implies
\ln\widehat{W}_{T}^{-1}\preceq I\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}
and
furthermore,

(q_{t,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}-q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top})

is positive semi-definite.
Thus, Lemma
8.2
, gives the result.

The expression for
2
can be bounded as

\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
