}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\mathrm{Tr}(q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}\ln(V_{t}\widehat{W}_{t}^{-1}))\leq q_{t-1,i}\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\end{array}

where the equality is due to the fact that
V_{t}
and
\widehat{W}_{t}

have the same eigenvectors.
The inequality follows since
\ln(V_{t}\widehat{W}_{t}^{-1})\preceq I\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
, due to the update in
Eq.(
32b
),
while
q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}
is
positive semi-definite. Thus Lemma
8.2
gives the result.

The bound
3
can be expressed as:

\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.54 0 C 9.54 5.27 5.27 9.54 0 9.54 C -5.27 9.54 -9.54 5.27 -9.54 0 C -9.54 -5.27 -5.27 -9.54 0 -9.54 C 5.27 -9.54 9.54 -5.27 9.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\mathrm{Tr}\big((-q_{t,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}+q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top})\ln V_{t}\big)\leq 0

Here, the inequality follows since

\ln V_{t}\preceq 0
and
and

(-q_{t,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top}+q_{t-1,i}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}\mathbf{\tilde{d}_{i}}^{\top})

is positive semi-definite.
Thus, Lemma
8.2
gives the result.

For
4
, we have
\hbox to14.18pt{\vbox to14.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.09111pt\lower-7.09111pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.54 0 C 9.54 5.27 5.27 9.54 0 9.54 C -5.27 9.54 -9.54 5.27 -9.54 0 C -9.54 -5.27 -5.27 -9.54 0 -9.54 C 5.27 -9.54 9.54 -5.27 9.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leq q_{t,i}\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
,
which follows the same argument used to bound the term
2
.

Thus,
\bar{A}
can be upper bounded as follows:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{A}\leq\underbrace{\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}\geq q_{t-1,i}}(q_{t,i}-q_{t-1,i})}_{=D_{TV}(\mathbf{q_{t}},\mathbf{q_{t-1}})}\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+\underbrace{\Big(\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}\geq q_{t-1,i}}q_{t-1,i}+\sum\limits_{i:q_{t,i}<q_{t-1,i}}q_{t,i}\Big)}_{=\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}-D_{TV}(\mathbf{q_{t}},\mathbf{q_{t-1}})}\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\end{array}

Then we telescope the
\bar{B}
term, substitute the above inequality for
\bar{A}
into Eq.(
100
),
and sum over
t=2,\dots,T
to give:

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\Big(-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln\widehat{W}_{t})+\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})\Big)\leq m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+\Big(\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}-m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\Big)\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{1}\ln V_{2})\end{array}

Adding the
t=1
term to the above inequality, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln\widehat{W}_{t})+\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})\Big)\leq\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}\ln(n)+m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+\Big(\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{q_{t}}\right\|_{1}-m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})\Big)\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\end{array}

For the above inequality, we set
Q_{t}=Q\in\mathscr{B}_{n-k}^{n}
for
t=r,\dots,s
and
0
elsewhere,
which makes
\mathbf{q_{t}}=\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{n-k}^{n}
for
t=r,\dots,s
and
0
elsewhere.
If
r\geq 2
,
m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})=1
, and
\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}=0
.
If
r=1
,
m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})=0
, and
\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}=1
.
Thus,
m(\mathbf{q_{1:T}})+\left\|\mathbf{q_{1}}\right\|_{1}=1
, and the right part can be upper bounded by

\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+T\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
.

The rest of the steps follow exactly the same as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
:

Proof.

We first deal with the term
\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})
.
According to the update in Eq.(
37a
), we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})=\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(Q_{t}\ln\Big(\frac{\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})}{\mathrm{Tr}(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t}))}\Big)\bigg)=\mathrm{Tr}\big(Q_{t}(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)-\ln\Big(\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)\Big),\end{array}

since
Q_{t}\in\mathscr{B}_{1}^{n}
and
\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t})=1
.

As a result, we have

\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln V_{t+1})-\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}\ln Y_{t})

=

-\eta\mathrm{Tr}(Q_{t}C_{t})-\ln\Big(\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)\Big)
.

Thus, to prove the inequality in Lemma
4.3
,
it is enough to prove the following inequality

\eta\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})-\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}+\ln\Big(\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)\Big)\leq 0

Before we proceed, we need the following lemmas:

Lemma 8.3

(Golden-Thompson inequality)
.

For any symmetric matrices
A
and
B
, the following inequality holds:

\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(A+B)\big)\leq\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(A)\exp(B)\big)

Lemma 8.4

(Lemma 2.1 in
[
8
]
)
.

For any symmetric matrix
A
such that
0\preceq A\preceq I
and any
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
the following holds:

\exp\big(A\rho_{1}+(I-A)\rho_{2}\big)\preceq A\exp(\rho_{1})+(I-A)\exp(\rho_{2})

Then we apply the Golden-Thompson inequality to the term
\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)
,
which gives us the inequality below:

\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)\leq\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}\exp(-\eta C_{t})).

For the term
\exp(-\eta C_{t})
,
by applying the Lemma
8.4
with
\rho_{1}=-\eta
and
\rho_{2}=0
,
we have the following inequality:

\exp(-\eta C_{t})\preceq I-C_{t}(1-\exp(-\eta)).

Thus, we have

\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}\exp(-\eta C_{t}))\leq 1-\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})(1-\exp(-\eta)),

and

\mathrm{Tr}\big(\exp(\ln Y_{t}-\eta C_{t})\big)\leq 1-\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})(1-\exp(-\eta)),

since
Y_{t}\in\mathscr{B}_{1}^{n}
and
\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t})=1
.

Thus, it is enough to prove the following inequality

\eta\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})-\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}+\ln\Big(1-\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})(1-\exp(-\eta))\Big)\leq 0

Since
\ln(1-x)\leq-x
, we have

\ln\Big(1-\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})(1-\exp(-\eta))\Big)\leq-\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})(1-\exp(-\eta)).

Thus, it suffices to prove the following inequality:

\big(\eta-1+\exp(-\eta)\big)\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})-\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}\leq 0

Note that by using convexity
of
\exp(-\eta)
,
\eta-1+\exp(-\eta)\geq 0
.

By applying Lemma
8.2
with
A=Y_{t}
,
B=C_{t}
, and
C=I
,
we have
\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t}C_{t})\leq\mathrm{Tr}(Y_{t})=1
.
Thus, when
\eta\geq 0
, it is enough to prove the following inequality

\eta-1+\exp(-\eta)-\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}\leq 0.

This inequality follows from convexity of

\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}-\exp(-\eta)
over
\eta\geq 0
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.6
:

Proof.

First, since
0\preceq C_{t}\preceq I
, we have
\max_{i,j}|C_{t}(i,j)|

\leq 1
.

Before we proceed, we need the following lemma from
[
9
]
:

Lemma 8.5

(Lemma 1 in
[
9
]
)
.

Let
\max_{i,j}|C_{t}(i,j)|\leq\frac{r}{2}
, then
for any
\mathbf{u_{t}}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}
, any constants
a
and
b
such that
0\leq a\leq\frac{b}{1+rb}
,
and
\eta=\frac{2b}{1+rb}
, we have

a\mathbf{y_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{y_{t}}-b\mathbf{u_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{u_{t}}\leq d(\mathbf{u_{t}},\mathbf{y_{t}})-d(\mathbf{u_{t}},\mathbf{v_{t+1}})

Now we apply Lemma
8.5

under the conditions
r=2
,
a=\frac{b}{2b+1}
,
\eta=2a
,
and
b=\frac{c}{2}
.

Recall that
d(\mathbf{u_{t}},\mathbf{y_{t}})-d(\mathbf{u_{t}},\mathbf{v_{t+1}})

=

\sum_{i}u_{t,i}\ln\Big(\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{y_{t,i}}\Big)
.
Combining this with the inequality in Lemma
8.5

and the fact that
\left\|\mathbf{u_{t}}\right\|_{1}=1
,
we have

a\left\|\mathbf{u_{t}}\right\|_{1}\mathbf{y_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{y_{t}}-b\mathbf{u_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{u_{t}}\leq\sum_{i}u_{t,i}\ln\Big(\frac{v_{t+1,i}}{y_{t,i}}\Big)

Note that the above inequality is also true when
\mathbf{u_{t}}=0
.

Note that the right side of the above inequality
is the same as the right part of the Eq.(
96
)
in the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

As a result, we will use the same steps as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
Then we will set
\mathbf{u_{t}}=\mathbf{u}=\operatornamewithlimits{argmin}_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{q}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{q}

for
t=r,\dots,s
, and
0
elsewhere. Summing from
t=1
up to
T
,
gives the following inequality:

a\big[\sum_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{y_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{y_{t}}\big]-b\big[\min_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}}\sum_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{u}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{u}\big]\leq\ln\frac{n}{\alpha}+T\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}

Since
\alpha=1/(T+1)
,
T\ln\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\leq 1
.
Then the above inequality becomes

a\big[\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{y_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{y_{t}}\big]-b\big[\min_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{u}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{u}\big]\leq\ln\big((1+T)n\big)+1

Plugging in the expressions of
a=c/(2c+2)
,
b=c/2
, and
c=\frac{\sqrt{2\ln\big((1+T)n\big)+2}}{\sqrt{L}}
we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{y_{t}}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{y_{t}}-\min_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}}\sum\limits_{t=r}^{s}\mathbf{u}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{u}&\leq c\Big[\min_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}C_{t}\mathbf{u}\Big]+2\frac{c+1}{c}\big(\ln\big((1+T)n\big)+1\big)\\
&\leq cL+2\frac{c+1}{c}\big(\ln\big((1+T)n\big)+1\big)\\
&=2\sqrt{2L\Big(\ln\big((1+T)n\big)+1\Big)}+2\ln\big((1+T)n\big)\end{array}

Since the inequality holds for any
1\leq r\leq s\leq T
, the proof is
concluded by maximizing over
[r,s]
on the left.
∎

Chapter 9
Online Convex Optimization for Cumulative Constraints

Appendix 9.A
Toy Example Results

The results including different
T
up to
20000
are shown in Fig.
6
,
whose results are averaged over 10 random sequences of
\{c_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
.
Since the standard deviations are small, we only plot the mean results.

From Fig.
3
we can see that the trajectories generated by
Clipped-OGD

follows the boundary very tightly until reaching the optimal point.
which is also reflected by the Fig.
6
(a) of the clipped long-term constraint violation.
For the
OGD
, its trajectory oscillates a lot around the boundary of
the actual constraint.
And if we examine the clipped and non-clipped constraint violation in Fig.
6
(b),
we find that although the clipped constraint violation is very high, its non-clipped one is very small.
This verifies the statement we make in the beginning that the big constraint violation at one time step is canceled out
by the strictly feasible constraint at the other time step.
For the
A-OGD
, its trajectory in Fig.
3
violates the constraint most of the time,
and this violation actually contributes to the lower objective regret shown in Fig.
6
.

Figure 6:
Toy Example Results:
(a): Clipped Long-term Constraint Violation.
(b): Long-term Constraint Violation.
(c): Cumulative Regret of the Loss function

Appendix 9.B
Proofs

Proof of Lemma
5.2

Proof.

Recall that the update for
\theta_{t+1}
is

\theta_{t+1}=\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\Big(\theta_{t}-\eta\partial_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-\frac{[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma}\partial_{\theta}([g(\theta_{t})]_{+})\Big)

(101)

Let
y_{t}=\theta_{t}-\eta\partial_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-\frac{[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma}\partial_{\theta}([g(\theta_{t})]_{+})
.

We first need to show that
g(\theta_{t+1})\leq g(y_{t})
.
Without loss of generality, let us assume that
y_{t}
is not in the set
\mathcal{B}
.
From convexity we have
g(y_{t})\geq g(\theta_{t+1})+\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(y_{t}-\theta_{t+1})
.
From non-expansiveness of the projection operator, we have that

(y_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}(\theta-\theta_{t+1})\leq 0
for
\theta\in\mathcal{B}
.
Let
\theta=\theta_{t+1}-\epsilon_{0}\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t+1})
with
\epsilon_{0}
small enough to make
\theta\in\mathcal{B}
.
We have
-\epsilon_{0}(y_{t}-\theta_{t+1})^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t+1})\leq 0
.
Then we have
g(\theta_{t+1})\leq g(y_{t})
.

As a result, if
g(y_{t})
is upper bounded, then so is
g(\theta_{t+1})
,
where
\theta_{t+1}=\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(y_{t})
.
If
T
is large enough,

\eta\left\|\partial_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta_{t})\right\|
would be very small.
Thus, we can use
0
-order Taylor expansion for differentiable
g(\theta)
as below:

\begin{array}[]{ll}g(y_{t})=g\Big(\theta_{t}-\eta\partial_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-\frac{[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma}\partial_{\theta}([g(\theta_{t})]_{+})\Big)\par\leq g\Big(\theta_{t}-\frac{[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma}\partial_{\theta}([g(\theta_{t})]_{+})\Big)+C\eta\end{array}

(102)

where
C
is a constant determined by the Taylor expansion remainder, as well as
the bound
\|\partial_{\theta}[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}\|\|\partial_{\theta}f(\theta_{t})\|\leq G^{2}
.

Set
\epsilon=(2C\sigma R^{2}\eta)^{1/3}=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}})
. We will show that if
g(\theta_{t})<\epsilon
, then
g(\theta_{t+1})\leq\epsilon+O(1/\sqrt{T})=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}})
. We will also show that if
g(\theta_{t})\geq\epsilon
,
then
g(\theta_{t+1})\leq g(\theta_{t})
. It follows then by induction that if

g(\theta_{1})<\epsilon
, then
g(\theta_{t})=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}})
for all

t
. We prove these
inequalities in three cases. Since
g(\theta_{t+1})\leq g(y_{t})
, it suffices
to bound
g(y_{t})
.

Case 1:
g(\theta_{t})\leq 0
.
In this case, the inequality for

g(y_{t})
, (
102
), becomes

g(y_{t})\leq g(\theta_{t})+C\eta\leq C\eta=O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}})

Case 2:
0<g(\theta_{t})<\epsilon
.
Since
[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}=g(\theta_{t})
, the bound on
g(y_{t})
becomes

g(y_{t})\leq g\Big(\theta_{t}-\frac{g(\theta_{t})}{\sigma}\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\Big)+C\eta

(103)

We will bound the right using standard methods from gradient descent proofs.
Since
g
is convex and
\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta)
has Lipschitz constant,
L
,
we have the inequality:

g(y)\leq g(x)+\nabla_{x}g(x)^{\top}(y-x)+\frac{L}{2}\|y-x\|^{2}

(104)

for all
x
and
y

[
65
]
.

Recall that

\epsilon=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}})
. Assume that
T
is sufficiently
large so that
\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma}<\frac{L\epsilon}{2\sigma}<1
.
Applying
(
104
) with
x=\theta_{t}
and

y=\theta_{t}-\frac{g(\theta_{t})}{\sigma}\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})
gives

\displaystyle g(y_{t})

\displaystyle\leq g\Big(\theta_{t}-\frac{[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma}\partial_{\theta}(g(\theta_{t}))\Big)+C\eta

(105)

\displaystyle\leq g(\theta_{t})-\frac{g(\theta_{t})}{\sigma}(1-\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma})\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}+C\eta

(106)

\displaystyle\leq g(\theta_{t})+C\eta=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}}).

(107)

where the third bound follows since
1-\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma}>0
.

Case 3:
g(\theta_{t})\geq\epsilon
.

A case can arise such that
g(\theta_{t-1})<\epsilon
but an additive term of order

O(\frac{1}{T^{1/2}})
leads to
\epsilon\leq g(\theta_{t})\leq\epsilon+C\eta=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}})
. We will now show that no further
increases are possible by bounding the final two terms of
(
106
) as

-\frac{g(\theta_{t})}{\sigma}(1-\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma})\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}+C\eta\leq 0\iff C\eta\leq\frac{g(\theta_{t})}{\sigma}(1-\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma})\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}.

(108)

Now, we
lower-bound the terms on the right of (
108
). Since
\epsilon+C\eta=O(\frac{1}{T^{1/6}})
, we have that for sufficiently large
T
,

1-\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma}\geq 1-\frac{L(\epsilon+C\eta)}{2\sigma}\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Further note that by convexity,
g(0)\geq g(\theta_{t})-\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})^{\top}\theta_{t}
. Since we assume that
0
is feasible,
we have that

\epsilon\leq g(\theta_{t})\leq\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})^{\top}\theta_{t}\leq\|\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\|\|\theta_{t}\|\leq\|\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\|R.

The final inequality follows since
\theta_{t}\in\mathcal{B}
. Thus, we have
the following bound for the right of (
108
):

\frac{g(\theta_{t})}{\sigma}(1-\frac{Lg(\theta_{t})}{2\sigma})\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g(\theta_{t})\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{2\sigma R^{2}}=C\eta.

The final equality follows by the definition of
\epsilon
.
∎

Proof of the Proposition
5.1
:

Proof.

From the construction of
\bar{g}(\theta)
, we have the

\bar{g}(\theta)\geq\max\limits_{i}g_{i}(\theta)
. Thus, if we can upper bound the
\bar{g}(\theta)
,

g_{i}(\theta)
will automatically be upper bounded.
In order to use Lemma
5.2
, we need to make sure
the following conditions are satisfied:

•

\bar{g}(\theta)
is convex and differentiable.

•

\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|
is upper bounded.

•

\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|_{2}
is upper bounded,
where
\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)

is the Hessian matrix.

The first condition is satisfied due to the formula of
\bar{g}(\theta)
.
To examine the second one, we have

\nabla_{\theta}\bar{g}(\theta)=\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\Bigg]

\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{\big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\big)^{2}}\left\|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{m\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}(\exp g_{i}(\theta))^{2}\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\right\|^{2}}{\big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\big)^{2}}\leq mG^{2}\end{array}

Thus,
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|\leq\sqrt{m}G
and the second condition is satisfied.

For
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|_{2}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)=&\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}g_{i}(\theta)+\exp g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)^{\top}\Bigg]}_{A}\\
&-\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\Big)\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)^{\top}\Big)}_{B}\end{array}

To upper bound
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|_{2}
, which is

\max\limits_{u^{\top}u=1}u^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)u=\max\limits_{u^{\top}u=1}u^{\top}Au-u^{\top}Bu\leq\max\limits_{u^{\top}u=1}u^{\top}Au

where the inequality is due to the fact that
B\succeq 0
.

Thus, we have
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\bar{g}(\theta)\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|A\right\|_{2}
.
For the
\left\|A\right\|_{2}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\left\|A\right\|_{2}&=\max\limits_{u^{\top}u=1}u^{\top}Au\leq\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\max\limits_{u^{\top}u=1}\exp g_{i}(\theta)u^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}g_{i}(\theta)u\Big)\\
&\quad\quad+\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\max\limits_{u^{\top}u=1}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)^{\top}u\right\|^{2}\Big)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)(L_{i}+\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\right\|^{2})\Big)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)}\Big(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\exp g_{i}(\theta)\Big)(\bar{L}+G^{2})=\bar{L}+G^{2}\end{array}

where the first inequality comes from the optimality definition, the second inequality comes from the upper bound for each

\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{\prime\prime}g_{i}(\theta)\right\|_{2}
and the Cauchy - Schwarz inequality,
and the last inequality comes from the fact that
\bar{L}=\max L_{i}
and
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}g_{i}(\theta)\right\|
is upper bounded by
G
.
Thus, the last condition is also satisfied.

∎

Proof of the Proposition
5.2
:

Proof.

From Theorem
5.1
, we know that
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\Big)^{2}\leq O(\sqrt{T})
.
By using the inequality
(y_{1}+y_{2}+...+y_{n})^{2}\leq n(y_{1}^{2}+y_{2}^{2}+...+y_{n}^{2})
,
setting
y_{i}
being equal to
[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
, and
n=T
, we have

\Big(\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\Big)^{2}\leq T\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\Big)^{2}\leq O(T^{3/2})
.
Then we obtain that
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\leq O(T^{3/4})
.
Because
g_{i}(\theta_{t})\leq[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
, we also have
g_{i}(\theta_{t})\leq O(T^{3/4})
.
∎

Proof of the Proposition
5.3
:

Proof.

Since we only change the step size for Algorithm
9
,
the previous result in Lemma
5.1
and part of the proof
up to Eq.(
50
) in Theorem
5.1

can be used without any changes.

First, let us rewrite the Eq.(
50
):

\begin{array}[]{rl}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\Big)&+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}}{\sigma\eta}\Big(1-\frac{(m+1)G^{2}}{2\sigma}\Big)\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2\eta}+\frac{\eta T}{2}(m+1)G^{2}\end{array}

(109)

By plugging in the definition of
\alpha
,
\eta
, and that
\frac{([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}}{\sigma\eta}\alpha\geq 0
,
we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\Big)&\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2}T^{\beta}+\frac{(m+1)G^{2}}{2}T^{1-\beta}=O(T^{max\{\beta,1-\beta\}})\end{array}

As argued in the proof of Theorem
5.1
,
we have the following inequalities with the help of

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(\theta^{*})\Big)\geq-FT
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}}{\sigma\eta}\alpha\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2}T^{\beta}+\frac{(m+1)G^{2}}{2}T^{1-\beta}+FT\\
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq\frac{\sigma}{\alpha}(\frac{R^{2}}{2}+\frac{(m+1)G^{2}}{2}T^{1-2\beta}+FT^{1-\beta})\end{array}

(110)

Then we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\leq\sqrt{T\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big([g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\Big)^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{T\sigma}{\alpha}\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{2}+\frac{(m+1)G^{2}}{2}T^{1-2\beta}+FT^{1-\beta}\Big)}=O(T^{1-\beta/2})\end{array}

∎

It is also interesting to figure out why
[
42
]

cannot have this user-defined trade-off benefit.
From
[
42
]
, the key inequality in obtaining their conclusions is:

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x^{*})\Big)+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Big[\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}g_{i}(x_{t})\Big]_{+}^{2}}{2(\sigma\eta T+m/\eta)}\\
\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2\eta}+\frac{\eta T}{2}\Big((m+1)G^{2}+2mD^{2}\Big)\end{array}

(111)

The main difference between Eq.(
111
) and Eq.(
109
)
is in the denominator of
\frac{\Big[\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}g_{i}(x_{t})\Big]_{+}^{2}}{2(\sigma\eta T+m/\eta)}
.
Eq.(
111
) has the form
(\sigma\eta T+m/\eta)
,
while Eq.(
109
) has the form
(\sigma\eta)
.
The coupled
\eta
and
1/\eta
prevents Eq.(
111
) from arriving this user-defined trade-off.

The next proofs of the Proposition
5.4
and
5.5

show how we can use our proposed Lagrangian function in Eq.(
46
)
to make the algorithms in
[
42
]
and
[
43
]

to have the clipped long-term constraint violation bounds.

Proof of the Proposition
5.4
:

Proof sktech.

If we look into the proof of Lemma 2 and Proposition 3 in
[
42
]
,
the new Lagrangian formula does not lead to any difference,
which means that the
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)
defined in Eq. (
46
)
is also valid for the drawn conclusions.
Then in the proof of Theorem 4 in
[
42
]
, we can change
g_{i}(\theta_{t})

to
[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
.
The maximization for
\lambda
over the range
[0,+\infty)
is also valid,
since
[g_{i}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
automatically satisfies this requirement.
Thus, the claimed bounds hold.
∎

Proof of the Proposition
5.5
:

Proof sktech.

The previous augmented Lagrangian formula
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)
used in
[
43
]
is:

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)=f_{t}(\theta)+\lambda g(\theta)-\frac{\phi_{t}}{2}\lambda^{2}

The Lemma 1 in
[
43
]
is the upper bound of
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})
.
The proof does not make any difference between formula (
9.B
) and (
55
).
So we can still have the same conclusion of Lemma 1.
The Lemma 2 in
[
43
]
is the lower bound of
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta^{*},\lambda_{t})
.
Since it only uses the fact that
g(\theta^{*})\leq 0
, which is also true for
[g(\theta^{*})]_{+}
,
we can have the same result with
g(\theta_{t})
being replaced with
[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}
.
The Lemma 3 in
[
43
]
is free of
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)
formula, so it is also true for the new formula.
The Lemma 4 in
[
43
]
is the result of Lemma 1-3,
so it is also valid if we change
g(\theta_{t})
to
[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}
.
Then the conclusion of Theorem 1 in
[
43
]
is valid for
[g(\theta_{t})]_{+}
as well.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3
:

Proof.

Due to the non-expansiveness of the projection in Eq. (
59b
),
we can get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}&\leq\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}+\eta\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}\\
&=\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}+\eta^{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}+2\eta\langle z_{t}-\theta_{t},\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\rangle\end{array}

which can be reformulated as

\langle\theta_{t}-z_{t},\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\rangle\leq\frac{1}{2\eta}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}

(112)

Due to the convexity of
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)
in terms of
\theta
, we have

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\langle\theta_{t}-z_{t},\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\rangle

(113)

Plugging Eq. (
113
) into Eq. (
112
), we get

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2\eta}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}

(114)

Next, we analyze the term
\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}&=\|z_{t}-z_{t+1}+z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}\\
&=\|z_{t}-z_{t+1}\|^{2}+2\langle z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1},z_{t}-z_{t+1}\rangle+\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}\end{array}

(115)

Since both
\theta_{1}^{T}
and
z_{1}^{T}
are in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
,
\|\theta_{t+1}-z_{t+1}\|\leq D

and
\langle z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1},z_{t}-z_{t+1}\rangle\geq-D\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|

due to the assumption that the diameter of
\mathcal{S}_{0}
is
D
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Thus, Eq. (
115
) can be lower bounded as

\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}\geq-2D\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}

(116)

Plug the above inequality into Eq. (
114
) gives

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\frac{D}{\eta}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})

For
\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}

=

\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta_{t})+\lambda_{t}\nabla_{\theta}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\|^{2}
,
it can be upper bounded by
2G^{2}+2G^{2}\lambda_{t}^{2}

due to the fact that
\|a+b\|^{2}\leq 2\|a\|^{2}+2\|b\|^{2}

and both
\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{t}(\theta_{t})\|
and
\|\nabla_{\theta}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\|
are upper bounded by
G
.
Plugging this upper bound into the above inequality gives

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2\eta}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{D}{\eta}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\eta G^{2}\lambda_{t}^{2}+\eta G^{2}

Expanding the left part of the above inequality and using
\lambda_{t}=\frac{[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma\eta}

results in

\begin{array}[]{l}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})+\frac{([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}}{\sigma\eta}(1-\frac{G^{2}}{\sigma})\\
\leq\frac{1}{2\eta}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{D}{\eta}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\eta G^{2}+\frac{[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}}{\sigma\eta}\end{array}

Since
\sigma=2G^{2}
, and
[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
is upper bounded by
F
,
we can get:

f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})+\frac{([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}}{4G^{2}\eta}\leq\frac{1}{2\eta}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{D}{\eta}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\eta G^{2}+\frac{F[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}}{2G^{2}\eta}

Summing over
t=1
to
T
, setting
z_{T+1}=z_{T}
, and using
\|z_{1}-x_{1}\|^{2}\leq D^{2}
gives

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\frac{1}{4G^{2}\eta}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta}+\frac{DV}{\eta}+G^{2}\eta T+\frac{F}{2G^{2}\eta}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}

According to the definition of the sequence
z_{1}^{T}
, the number of
[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}=0
is
K
.
With the bound of
[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}\leq F
,

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}\leq(T-K)F
.

Thus, the above inequality can be reformulated as

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\frac{1}{4G^{2}\eta}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta}+\frac{DV}{\eta}+G^{2}\eta T+\frac{F^{2}}{2G^{2}\eta}(T-K)

If we plug in the expression of
\eta=O(\sqrt{\frac{T-K+1+V}{T}})
, we get

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\frac{1}{4G^{2}\eta}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq O(\sqrt{T(T-K+1+V)})

Since
\frac{1}{4G^{2}\eta}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\geq 0
,

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\leq O(\sqrt{T(T-K+1+V)})
.

Also,
\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\geq-FT
.
Then
\frac{1}{4G^{2}\eta}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq O(T)
,
which results in
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}([g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+})^{2}\leq O(\sqrt{T(T-K+1+V)})
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.4
:

Proof.

Since the update is the same as the one in Eq. (
59
) except the time-dependent parameters,
we have the same inequality as in Eq. (
112
):

\langle\theta_{t}-z_{t},\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\rangle\leq\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}

(117)

Since
f_{t}
is
\ell
strongly convex,
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)
is also
\ell
strongly convex as:

\langle\theta_{t}-z_{t},\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\rangle\geq\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})+\frac{\ell}{2}\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}

Plugging the above inequality into Eq. (
117
) gives:

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{\ell}{2}\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}

Due to the lower bound for
\|z_{t}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2}
as in Eq. (
116
)
and the upper bound for
\|\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\|^{2}
as
2G^{2}+2G^{2}\lambda_{t}^{2}

as in the proof of Theorem
5.3
,
we get

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\frac{D}{\eta_{t}}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})-\frac{\ell}{2}\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}+\eta_{t}G^{2}+\eta_{t}G^{2}\lambda_{t}^{2}

Due to the concavity of
\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta,\lambda)
in terms of
\lambda
,
we have

\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda)-\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t})\leq\langle\nabla_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}_{t}(\theta_{t},\lambda_{t}),\lambda-\lambda_{t}\rangle=0

where the equality is due to the update of
\lambda_{t}
.

Adding the above two inequalities and reformulating with
\phi_{t}=2G^{2}\eta_{t}
and
g_{t}(\theta_{t})\leq F
gives

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})+\lambda[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}-\frac{\phi_{t}}{2}\lambda^{2}&\leq\frac{D}{\eta_{t}}\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|+\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}(\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}-\|z_{t+1}-\theta_{t+1}\|^{2})\\
&\quad-\frac{\ell}{2}\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}+\eta_{t}G^{2}+\frac{F}{\phi_{t}}[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}\end{array}

Summing over from
t=1
to
T
gives

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\lambda\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\phi_{t}\\
\leq D\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\|z_{t+1}-z_{t}\|}{\eta_{t}}+(\frac{1}{2\eta_{1}}-\frac{\ell}{2})\|z_{1}-\theta_{1}\|^{2}+G^{2}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\eta_{t}+\sum\limits_{t=2}^{T}(\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t-1}}-\frac{\ell}{2})\|z_{t}-\theta_{t}\|^{2}+F\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{[g_{t}(z_{t})]_{+}}{\phi_{t}}\end{array}

Since
\eta_{t}=\frac{1-\gamma}{\ell(1-\gamma^{t})}
and
\phi_{t}=2G^{2}\eta_{t}
,

\frac{1}{2\eta_{1}}-\frac{\ell}{2}=0

and

\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t-1}}-\frac{\ell}{2}\leq 0
.
Also,
\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}\leq\frac{\ell}{1-\gamma}

and
\frac{1}{\phi_{t}}\leq\frac{1}{2G^{2}}\frac{\ell}{1-\gamma}
.

Thus, by setting
z_{T+1}=z_{T}
and using the fact that
z_{1}^{T}\in V_{K}(z_{1}^{T})
, the above inequality can be simplified as

\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\lambda\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\phi_{t}\\
\leq\frac{D\ell}{1-\gamma}V+\frac{G^{2}(1-\gamma)}{\ell}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}+\frac{F^{2}\ell}{2G^{2}(1-\gamma)}(T-K)\end{array}

Maximizing the LHS over
\lambda
, we get
\lambda=\frac{\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}}{\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\phi_{t}}
,
which gives

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Big(\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\Big)^{2}}{\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\phi_{t}}\leq\Big(D\ell V+\frac{\ell F^{2}(T-K)}{2G^{2}}\Big)\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\frac{G^{2}(1-\gamma)}{\ell}\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}

For the first term on the RHS,
since
\frac{1}{1-\gamma}=2\sqrt{\frac{(D+1)T}{\max\{V+(T-K),\log^{2}T/T\}}}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{(D+1)T}{V+(T-K)}}
,
it can be upper bounded by
O(\sqrt{T(V+T-K)})
.

For the second term on the RHS,
since
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{t}}\leq O(T)
according to the proof of Corollary 3 in
[
38
]
,
it can be upper bounded by
\max\{O(\sqrt{T(V+T-K)}),O(\log T)\}
.

Thus,

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Big(\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}\Big)^{2}}{\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\phi_{t}}\leq\max\{O(\sqrt{T(V+T-K)}),O(\log T)\}

As a result,
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\leq\max\{O(\sqrt{T(V+T-K)}),O(\log T)\}
.

Since
\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}\Big(f_{t}(\theta_{t})-f_{t}(z_{t})\Big)\geq-FT
,
the upper bound for

\sum\limits_{t=1}^{T}[g_{t}(\theta_{t})]_{+}
is obtained by using the definition of
\phi_{t}
and the upper bound of it.

∎
